========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, yeah. Thanks. Um... well, I guess what really pushed me to come in is work. I’m a receptionist at a hotel downtown, and lately... I don't know. We actually just got these new sliding doors installed in the front, which you'd think would be quieter, but the sensors are all messed up. The contractor who put them in—I think his name was Gary, or maybe Greg, he had this really loud truck—he said they needed a week to "calibrate." I don't even know what that means for a piece of glass. I took a few engineering classes back in college before I dropped out, but I never learned anything about calibrating doors, so what do I know, right? 

But anyway, when they don't catch right, the doors just slam. And every time that happens, or even if someone just rolls one of those hard-shell suitcases really fast across the lobby tile, I just... I jump. I jump like it’s happening again. And then I'm just standing there, and it takes me a solid minute to get my heart back down to normal. It’s making it really hard to just do my job.

Um, most days? I mean... I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I do worry about things, sure. Like, the other day I was at the grocery store, the one on 4th Street, not the one by my apartment because their produce is always terrible. I swear, the last time I bought apples there they were bruised before I even put them in the bag. Anyway, I was at the good grocery store, and I realized I had left my reusable bags in the trunk, and I got so stressed out about having to pay for the paper ones. They charge ten cents a bag now! Which doesn't sound like a lot, but it adds up, you know? And I just stood there in the checkout line feeling like I couldn't breathe for a second because I was so mad at myself for forgetting them again. But... wait, what did you ask me? Am I anxious most days? I don't know, maybe. I just try to keep busy, mostly.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n2\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.10 - Post-traumatic stress disorder, unspecified, F40.00 - Agoraphobia, unspecified, F40.11 - Social phobia, generalized, F41.0 - Panic disorder [episodic paroxysmal anxiety], F41.1 - Generalized anxiety disorder, F42.0 - Obsessive-compulsive disorder, predominantly obsessional thoughts, F32.0 - Mild depressive episode\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, yeah. Thanks. Um... well, I guess what really pushed me to come in is work. I’m a receptionist at a hotel downtown, and lately... I don't know. We actually just got these new sliding doors installed in the front, which you'd think would be quieter, but the sensors are all messed up. The contractor who put them in—I think his name was Gary, or maybe Greg, he had this really loud truck—he said they needed a week to \"calibrate.\" I don't even know what that means for a piece of glass. I took a few engineering classes back in college before I dropped out, but I never learned anything about calibrating doors, so what do I know, right? \n\nBut anyway, when they don't catch right, the doors just slam. And every time that happens, or even if someone just rolls one of those hard-shell suitcases really fast across the lobby tile, I just... I jump. I jump like it’s happening again. And then I'm just standing there, and it takes me a solid minute to get my heart back down to normal. It’s making it really hard to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when you're not at work or dealing with the doors?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean... I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I do worry about things, sure. Like, the other day I was at the grocery store, the one on 4th Street, not the one by my apartment because their produce is always terrible. I swear, the last time I bought apples there they were bruised before I even put them in the bag. Anyway, I was at the good grocery store, and I realized I had left my reusable bags in the trunk, and I got so stressed out about having to pay for the paper ones. They charge ten cents a bag now! Which doesn't sound like a lot, but it adds up, you know? Um, avoiding places? I guess... well, yeah, actually. At work, I've started taking the back hallway whenever I can. Walking past that spot in the lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just really want to get away from it. 

Which is super annoying, honestly, because the back hallway is where they keep the extra recycling bins, and it always smells like stale beer and cardboard. I don't even know why it smells like beer, we work in an office! Unless someone from accounting is having a really good time on their lunch break. I wouldn't put it past Jim. He's always talking about these microbreweries he visits on the weekends. He brought in this IPA once for a holiday party, and it tasted like pine needles. I don't get the appeal of IPAs. Um, other places? Like stores and stuff? No, I don't think so. I still go out and do my normal errands. I actually went to Trader Joe's just yesterday after my shift. Anxious when I'm just relaxing? No, not really. If I'm at home on the couch watching TV or whatever, I'm usually pretty chill. I've been watching this new baking show lately, actually. It's one of those ones where they have to make these crazy elaborate cakes that look like real objects, you know? Like a cake that looks exactly like a pair of sneakers or a stack of books. It's wild. 

I tried baking a cake from scratch once for my mom's birthday a few years ago. I thought, how hard could it be? Just flour, sugar, eggs. But I accidentally used baking soda instead of baking powder, or maybe it was the other way around? Anyway, it came out completely flat and hard as a rock. You could have used it as a frisbee. We ended up just going to the grocery store and buying one of those sheet cakes with the overly sweet frosting. The frosting that stains your teeth blue for like two days? I don't even know what kind of food dye they put in that stuff, but it can't be good for you. It probably has the same chemicals as those car cleaning wipes I was talking about earlier. 

But yeah, anyway... I don't really feel on edge when I'm just hanging out at home. It's mostly just the jumping at loud noises at work. When I'm home, I'm fine.

Worry about having another one? Um, no, I don't think I really sit around worrying about it. Like I said, when I'm home, I'm just doing my thing. When I'm home, I'm fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about having another episode of intense anxiety or panic, even when you're feeling fine at the moment?\n\nPatient: Worry about having another one? Um, no, I don't think I really sit around worrying about it. Like I said, when I'm home, I'm just doing my thing. Like last night, I was trying to fix this wobbly leg on my coffee table. It's been driving me crazy for weeks. Every time I put a mug down, the whole thing shimmies. \n\nI bought this little kit of wood glue and those little wooden peg things—dowels, I think they're called? I got them at Home Depot. Have you ever gone to Home Depot on a Saturday morning? It is absolute chaos. People are walking around with these massive carts full of lumber, looking completely lost. I saw this one guy trying to fit a whole sheet of plywood into the back of a Honda Civic. Like, buddy, that is just physics. It's not going to happen. He had the trunk tied down with what looked like a shoelace. I was just waiting for it to fly out onto the highway and cause a ten-car pileup. \n\nI mean, people just don't think things through, you know? It's like my sister. She decided to foster three kittens all at once. Three! She lives in a tiny one-bedroom apartment. Now her curtains are basically shredded cheese and she hasn't slept in a week because they treat her head like a springboard at 3 AM. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, worrying about the panic thing. Yeah, no, I don't really stress about it happening again out of nowhere. It's just that one spot at work that gets me. Otherwise, I'm not really dwelling on it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel a general sense of anxiety or worry, even when you're not in a situation that's causing it, like a nagging feeling that something bad might happen?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 35 / receptionist_or_information_clerk / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A nagging feeling that something bad might happen? No, not really. I mean, I don't walk around feeling like the sky is falling or anything. I'm usually pretty focused on whatever I'm doing. Like today, I was trying to figure out this new software update on my phone. They always push these updates overnight, and then you wake up and suddenly everything looks different. 

The icons are all rounder, or the font is slightly smaller, and it takes me like three days just to figure out how to set my alarm again. And don't even get me started on the terms and conditions. Does anyone actually read those? It's like forty pages of legal jargon. You could be agreeing to hand over your firstborn child and you'd just click "Accept" because you want to get back to scrolling through Instagram. 

My buddy Dave actually got locked out of his email for a week because he clicked the wrong thing on one of those updates. He had to call customer service, and he was on hold for literally three hours. They played that same loop of elevator music the entire time. He said he was humming it in his sleep. I told him he should have just put it on speaker and gone about his day, but Dave is one of those guys who will just sit there staring at the phone like it's going to talk to him any second. 

Wait, what was the question? A nagging feeling? Oh, right. Yeah, no, I don't really have that. Unless I'm at work near that one area, I'm pretty much just going about my normal day without worrying about bad stuff happening.

He's always talking about these microbreweries he visits on the weekends. He brought in this IPA once for a holiday party, and it tasted like pine needles. I don't get the appeal of IPAs. Restless? Nah, I wouldn't say that. I'm usually pretty relaxed if I'm not at the front desk. Like this past weekend, I decided to finally clean out my hall closet. It's been a total disaster zone since I moved in.

Now her curtains are basically shredded cheese and she hasn't slept in a week because they treat her head like a springboard at 3 AM. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, worrying about the panic thing. Yeah, no, I don't really stress about it happening again out of nowhere. It's just that one spot at work that gets me. Otherwise, I'm not really dwelling on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a general sense of anxiety or worry, even when you're not in a situation that's causing it, like a nagging feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: A nagging feeling that something bad might happen? No, not really. I mean, I don't walk around feeling like the sky is falling or anything. I'm usually pretty focused on whatever I'm doing. Like today, I was trying to figure out this new software update on my phone. They always push these updates overnight, and then you wake up and suddenly everything looks different. \n\nThe icons are all rounder, or the font is slightly smaller, and it takes me like three days just to figure out how to set my alarm again. And don't even get me started on the terms and conditions. Does anyone actually read those? It's like forty pages of legal jargon. You could be agreeing to hand over your firstborn child and you'd just click \"Accept\" because you want to get back to scrolling through Instagram. \n\nMy buddy Dave actually got locked out of his email for a week because he clicked the wrong thing on one of those updates. He had to call customer service, and he was on hold for literally three hours. They played that same loop of elevator music the entire time. He said he was humming it in his sleep. I told him he should have just put it on speaker and gone about his day, but Dave is one of those guys who will just sit there staring at the phone like it's going to talk to him any second. \n\nWait, what was the question? A nagging feeling? Oh, right. Yeah, no, I don't really have that. Unless I'm at work near that one area, I'm pretty much just going about my normal day without worrying about bad stuff happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless, keyed up, or on edge more often than not, even when you’re not at work or dealing with the doors?\n\nPatient: Restless? Nah, I wouldn't say that. I'm usually pretty relaxed if I'm not at the front desk. Like this past weekend, I decided to finally clean out my hall closet. It's been a total disaster zone since I moved in. \n\nI actually found this old leather jacket I bought like ten years ago. I thought I looked so cool in it back then, like some kind of tough motorcycle guy, but I drive a 2012 Corolla, you know? It doesn't exactly give off the same vibe. Anyway, I tried the jacket on and it barely zipped. It's crazy how clothes just shrink in the closet over time. Or maybe it's all those grocery store sheet cakes I was talking about earlier. I ended up bagging it up with a bunch of other stuff and taking it down to the donation bin behind the shopping center. \n\nHave you ever actually looked at what people put in those bins? It says \"Clothing and Shoes Only\" in giant letters, but people just throw the weirdest stuff in there. I saw a toaster sticking out of the top of the chute. Like, who is donating a crusty old toaster to a clothing drive? It still had crumbs in it! And right next to it was a single rollerblade. Just one. What is someone supposed to do with one rollerblade? People are so weird. \n\nBut, uh, what were you asking? Oh, feeling keyed up. No, outside of work and dealing with those doors, I'm pretty mellow. I don't really get restless like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever experienced a traumatic event that you think might be connected to why you react so strongly to the doors slamming at work?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 35 / receptionist_or_information_clerk / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about two weeks, ever since that guest threatened me at the front desk.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A traumatic event? Um, well, yeah, I guess. I mean, this whole thing with the doors and getting all worked up, it's only been going on for about two weeks. It started right after this guy threatened me at the front desk. 

He came in trying to check in, but his reservation was for the wrong weekend. I tried to explain it to him, but he just went off. He started screaming, leaned over the counter, and told me he was going to wait for me in the parking lot after my shift. He slammed his hands down on the desk so hard my stapler flew off. 

Speaking of staplers, have you ever noticed how nobody ever buys their own stapler? It's like they just appear in offices. I've had the same red Swingline on my desk for three years, and I have no idea where it came from. It reminds me of that movie, Office Space, you know? With the guy who burns the building down because they took his stapler? That movie is a classic. My buddy Dave—the one with the email thing—he actually dressed up as that guy for Halloween one year. He carried around a red stapler and a mug all night. The problem was, nobody at the party had seen the movie, so they just thought he was a really boring office worker. He spent the whole night trying to explain the joke, which just made it worse. It's like when you have to explain a pun, it completely ruins it. 

But yeah, anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the guy at the desk. Yeah, ever since he threatened me and slammed his hands down, that's when this whole thing started. About two weeks ago.

Why would you want to drink something that tastes like a Christmas tree? I'm more of a lager guy myself. Just something simple, you know? Like, cold and crisp. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, the back hallway. I just use that now so I don't have to walk through the main lobby. It's just easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Besides avoiding parts of your workplace, have you found yourself avoiding other places or situations, like public transportation, stores, or social events, because you're worried about feeling anxious or having a panic-like reaction?\n\nPatient: Um, other places? Like stores and stuff? No, I don't think so. I still go out and do my normal errands. I actually went to Trader Joe's just yesterday after my shift. Though honestly, I probably *should* avoid that place just because of the parking lot. Have you ever noticed how they always make their parking lots incredibly tiny? It’s like a corporate requirement for them to open a store. And everyone drives these massive SUVs now. I saw this guy in a giant truck trying to back into a compact spot and he completely rolled over the landscaping. Just crushed these poor little bushes. \n\nAnd then inside, everyone goes absolutely crazy for the seasonal stuff. I just wanted my regular peanut butter pretzels, but people were practically fighting over the pumpkin spice ones. I mean, pumpkin spice is getting way out of hand, right? My neighbor’s dog, Buster, literally ate a whole stick of butter off their kitchen island last week, wrapper and all. He’s a golden retriever, so he’s not the sharpest tool in the shed, but still. The vet bill must have been ridiculous. \n\nWait, sorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask? Oh, right, avoiding things. No, I don't avoid the bus or the grocery store or hanging out with friends or anything. It's really just that one spot at work. Everything else is pretty much fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge even when there’s nothing specific going on to cause it, like when you’re at home relaxing or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Anxious when I'm just relaxing? No, not really. If I'm at home on the couch watching TV or whatever, I'm usually pretty chill. I've been watching this new baking show lately, actually. It's one of those ones where they have to make these crazy elaborate cakes that look like real objects, you know? Like a cake that looks exactly like a pair of sneakers or a stack of books. It's wild. \n\nI tried baking a cake from scratch once for my mom's birthday a few years ago. I thought, how hard could it be? Just flour, sugar, eggs. But I accidentally used baking soda instead of baking powder, or maybe it was the other way around? Anyway, it came out completely flat and hard as a rock. You could have used it as a frisbee. We ended up just going to the grocery store and buying one of those sheet cakes with the overly sweet frosting. The frosting that stains your teeth blue for like two days? He had the trunk tied down with what looked like a shoelace. Trouble sleeping? No, my sleep is fine. I'm usually out like a light. But, um, irritability? Yeah, maybe a little. I’m usually pretty careful with my coworkers, you know, try to keep things professional and friendly. But lately, I hear myself snapping short answers over little things. It's weird because I'm not usually like that. I usually try to be the helpful guy. Like last week, I helped this elderly couple figure out the vending machine in the lobby. Those things are getting ridiculously complicated. It’s not just putting in a dollar and pressing A4 anymore. Now they have these digital touchscreens, and you have to swipe a card, and then it asks if you want a receipt. Who wants a receipt for a bag of Doritos? Are you going to expense that? "Yes, accounting, here is my $1.75 charge for Cool Ranch." 

And the machines always glitch out anyway. This poor couple was just trying to get a bottle of water, and the machine kept asking for an email address to send a digital coupon. I ended up just using my own card and getting it for them because it was taking forever. 

But yeah, what were you asking? Oh, snapping at people. Yeah, I've been doing that a bit lately, just over stupid little things like confirmation numbers. As for being easily startled, it's really just the doors slamming that gets me.

I don't know. We actually just got these new sliding doors installed in the front, which you'd think would be quieter, but the sensors are all messed up. The contractor who put them in—I think his name was Gary, or maybe Greg, he had this really loud truck—he said they needed a week to \"calibrate.\" I don't even know what that means for a piece of glass. I took a few engineering classes back in college before I dropped out, but I never learned anything about calibrating doors, so what do I know, right? \n\nBut anyway, when they don't catch right, the doors just slam. And every time that happens, or even if someone just rolls one of those hard-shell suitcases really fast across the lobby tile, I just... I jump. I jump like it’s happening again. And then I'm just standing there, and it takes me a solid minute to get my heart back down to normal. It’s making it really hard to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when you're not at work or dealing with the doors?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean... I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I do worry about things, sure. Like, the other day I was at the grocery store, the one on 4th Street, not the one by my apartment because their produce is always terrible. I swear, the last time I bought apples there they were bruised before I even put them in the bag. Anyway, I was at the good grocery store, and I realized I had left my reusable bags in the trunk, and I got so stressed out about having to pay for the paper ones. They charge ten cents a bag now! Which doesn't sound like a lot, but it adds up, you know? And I just stood there in the checkout line feeling like I couldn't breathe for a second because I was so mad at myself for forgetting them again. But... wait, what did you ask me? Am I anxious most days? I don't know, maybe. I just try to keep busy, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried most days, even when there isn't a specific problem happening at the moment?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious when there isn't a problem? I mean... I don't think so? Not really. Like, if I'm just sitting at home watching TV, I'm usually fine. I've been rewatching that baking show, the British one. Have you seen it? It's so calming. Except when they have to make bread, because yeast is just... it's so unpredictable. I tried baking sourdough during the pandemic, like everyone else, right? I got the starter going, I named him Dough-Joe—like a dojo, but dough, you know? Anyway, he died after two weeks because I forgot to feed him. It smelled awful. My whole apartment smelled like vinegar for days. I had to open all the windows, which was a nightmare because the street cleaner comes by on Tuesdays and it kicks up so much dust. I swear they don't even use water in those trucks anymore, they just move the dirt from the street onto the sidewalk. But... sorry, what were we talking about? Am I anxious for no reason? No, I don't think I just sit around worrying about nothing. It's usually something specific, like the doors, or... or the grocery bags.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had sudden episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort that came on quickly and included symptoms like shortness of breath, chest pain, or dizziness?\n\nPatient: Sudden intense fear? Like, just out of nowhere with chest pain and dizziness? No, I don't think so. I mean, I get dizzy sometimes if I stand up from the couch too fast, but my sister is always telling me that's just because I don't drink enough water. \n\nShe actually bought me one of those giant water bottles for my birthday last month. You know the ones with the motivational quotes printed on the side? Like \"Keep Going!\" at 10 AM and \"Almost There!\" at 4 PM. It's ridiculous. Plus, it's so huge it doesn't even fit in my car's cup holder. I drive an older Civic, and the cup holders are just tiny. You can barely fit a regular coffee in them. I spilled a caramel iced coffee in there like three years ago and I swear the bottom is still sticky, no matter how many of those interior cleaning wipes I use on it. The lemon-scented ones? They don't even smell like lemons, they just smell like straight chemicals. I even tried using a Q-tip to get in the little edges, but it just shredded the cotton everywhere. \n\nBut... wait, sorry, I lost track. You were asking about chest pain? Yeah, no, I don't get anything like that. Just the jumping and the racing heart when the doors slam at work. But not just out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself avoiding certain places or situations because you're worried they might cause you to feel anxious or have a panic-like reaction?\n\nPatient: Um, avoiding places? I guess... well, yeah, actually. At work, I've started taking the back hallway whenever I can. Walking past that spot in the lobby where it happened... I don't know, my chest just gets all tight and I just really want to get away from it. \n\nWhich is super annoying, honestly, because the back hallway is where they keep the extra recycling bins, and it always smells like stale beer and cardboard. I don't even know why it smells like beer, we work in an office! Unless someone from accounting is having a really good time on their lunch break. I wouldn't put it past Jim. He's always talking about these microbreweries he visits on the weekends. He brought in this IPA once for a holiday party, and it tasted like pine needles. I don't get the appeal of IPAs. Why would you want to drink something that tastes like a Christmas tree? I'm more of a lager guy myself. Just something simple, you know? Like, cold and crisp. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, the back hallway. I just use that now so I don't have to walk through the main lobby. It's just easier that way.\n\nDoctor: Besides avoiding parts of your workplace, have you found yourself avoiding other places or situations, like public transportation, stores, or social events, because you're worried about feeling anxious or having a panic-like reaction?\n\nPatient: Um, other places? Like stores and stuff? No, I don't think so. I still go out and do my normal errands. I actually went to Trader Joe's just yesterday after my shift. Though honestly, I probably *should* avoid that place just because of the parking lot. Have you ever noticed how they always make their parking lots incredibly tiny? It’s like a corporate requirement for them to open a store. And everyone drives these massive SUVs now. I saw this guy in a giant truck trying to back into a compact spot and he completely rolled over the landscaping. Just crushed these poor little bushes. \n\nAnd then inside, everyone goes absolutely crazy for the seasonal stuff. I just wanted my regular peanut butter pretzels, but people were practically fighting over the pumpkin spice ones. I mean, pumpkin spice is getting way out of hand, right? I saw pumpkin spice dog treats the other day. Why would a dog care if its treat tastes like cinnamon and nutmeg? They eat dirt. My neighbor’s dog, Buster, literally ate a whole stick of butter off their kitchen island last week, wrapper and all. He’s a golden retriever, so he’s not the sharpest tool in the shed, but still. The vet bill must have been ridiculous. \n\nWait, sorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask? Oh, right, avoiding things. No, I don't avoid the bus or the grocery store or hanging out with friends or anything. It's really just that one spot at work. Everything else is pretty much fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge even when there’s nothing specific going on to cause it, like when you’re at home relaxing or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Anxious when I'm just relaxing? No, not really. If I'm at home on the couch watching TV or whatever, I'm usually pretty chill. I've been watching this new baking show lately, actually. It's one of those ones where they have to make these crazy elaborate cakes that look like real objects, you know? Like a cake that looks exactly like a pair of sneakers or a stack of books. It's wild. \n\nI tried baking a cake from scratch once for my mom's birthday a few years ago. I thought, how hard could it be? Just flour, sugar, eggs. But I accidentally used baking soda instead of baking powder, or maybe it was the other way around? Anyway, it came out completely flat and hard as a rock. You could have used it as a frisbee. We ended up just going to the grocery store and buying one of those sheet cakes with the overly sweet frosting. The frosting that stains your teeth blue for like two days? I don't even know what kind of food dye they put in that stuff, but it can't be good for you. It probably has the same chemicals as those car cleaning wipes I was talking about earlier. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... I don't really feel on edge when I'm just hanging out at home. It's mostly just the jumping at loud noises at work. When I'm home, I'm fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about having another episode of intense anxiety or panic, even when you're feeling fine at the moment?\n\nPatient: Worry about having another one? Um, no, I don't think I really sit around worrying about it. Like I said, when I'm home, I'm just doing my thing. Like last night, I was trying to fix this wobbly leg on my coffee table. It's been driving me crazy for weeks. Every time I put a mug down, the whole thing shimmies. \n\nI bought this little kit of wood glue and those little wooden peg things—dowels, I think they're called? I got them at Home Depot. Have you ever gone to Home Depot on a Saturday morning? It is absolute chaos. People are walking around with these massive carts full of lumber, looking completely lost. I saw this one guy trying to fit a whole sheet of plywood into the back of a Honda Civic. Like, buddy, that is just physics. It's not going to happen. He had the trunk tied down with what looked like a shoelace. I was just waiting for it to fly out onto the highway and cause a ten-car pileup. \n\nI mean, people just don't think things through, you know? It's like my sister. She decided to foster three kittens all at once. Three! She lives in a tiny one-bedroom apartment. Now her curtains are basically shredded cheese and she hasn't slept in a week because they treat her head like a springboard at 3 AM. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, worrying about the panic thing. Yeah, no, I don't really stress about it happening again out of nowhere. It's just that one spot at work that gets me. Otherwise, I'm not really dwelling on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a general sense of anxiety or worry, even when you're not in a situation that's causing it, like a nagging feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: A nagging feeling that something bad might happen? No, not really. I mean, I don't walk around feeling like the sky is falling or anything. I'm usually pretty focused on whatever I'm doing. Like today, I was trying to figure out this new software update on my phone. They always push these updates overnight, and then you wake up and suddenly everything looks different. \n\nThe icons are all rounder, or the font is slightly smaller, and it takes me like three days just to figure out how to set my alarm again. And don't even get me started on the terms and conditions. Does anyone actually read those? It's like forty pages of legal jargon. You could be agreeing to hand over your firstborn child and you'd just click \"Accept\" because you want to get back to scrolling through Instagram. \n\nMy buddy Dave actually got locked out of his email for a week because he clicked the wrong thing on one of those updates. He had to call customer service, and he was on hold for literally three hours. They played that same loop of elevator music the entire time. He said he was humming it in his sleep. I told him he should have just put it on speaker and gone about his day, but Dave is one of those guys who will just sit there staring at the phone like it's going to talk to him any second. \n\nWait, what was the question? A nagging feeling? Oh, right. Yeah, no, I don't really have that. Unless I'm at work near that one area, I'm pretty much just going about my normal day without worrying about bad stuff happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless, keyed up, or on edge more often than not, even when you’re not at work or dealing with the doors?\n\nPatient: Restless? Nah, I wouldn't say that. I'm usually pretty relaxed if I'm not at the front desk. Like this past weekend, I decided to finally clean out my hall closet. It's been a total disaster zone since I moved in. \n\nI actually found this old leather jacket I bought like ten years ago. I thought I looked so cool in it back then, like some kind of tough motorcycle guy, but I drive a 2012 Corolla, you know? It doesn't exactly give off the same vibe. Anyway, I tried the jacket on and it barely zipped. It's crazy how clothes just shrink in the closet over time. Or maybe it's all those grocery store sheet cakes I was talking about earlier. I ended up bagging it up with a bunch of other stuff and taking it down to the donation bin behind the shopping center. \n\nHave you ever actually looked at what people put in those bins? It says \"Clothing and Shoes Only\" in giant letters, but people just throw the weirdest stuff in there. I saw a toaster sticking out of the top of the chute. Like, who is donating a crusty old toaster to a clothing drive? It still had crumbs in it! And right next to it was a single rollerblade. Just one. What is someone supposed to do with one rollerblade? People are so weird. \n\nBut, uh, what were you asking? Oh, feeling keyed up. No, outside of work and dealing with those doors, I'm pretty mellow. I don't really get restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a traumatic event that you think might be connected to why you react so strongly to the doors slamming at work?\n\nPatient: A traumatic event? Um, well, yeah, I guess. I mean, this whole thing with the doors and getting all worked up, it's only been going on for about two weeks. It started right after this guy threatened me at the front desk. \n\nHe came in trying to check in, but his reservation was for the wrong weekend. I tried to explain it to him, but he just went off. He started screaming, leaned over the counter, and told me he was going to wait for me in the parking lot after my shift. He slammed his hands down on the desk so hard my stapler flew off. \n\nSpeaking of staplers, have you ever noticed how nobody ever buys their own stapler? It's like they just appear in offices. I've had the same red Swingline on my desk for three years, and I have no idea where it came from. It reminds me of that movie, Office Space, you know? With the guy who burns the building down because they took his stapler? That movie is a classic. My buddy Dave—the one with the email thing—he actually dressed up as that guy for Halloween one year. He carried around a red stapler and a mug all night. The problem was, nobody at the party had seen the movie, so they just thought he was a really boring office worker. He spent the whole night trying to explain the joke, which just made it worse. It's like when you have to explain a pun, it completely ruins it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the guy at the desk. Yeah, ever since he threatened me and slammed his hands down, that's when this whole thing started. About two weeks ago.\n\nDoctor: Since the incident at work, have you noticed any ongoing symptoms like trouble sleeping, irritability, or being easily startled beyond what you've already described?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? No, my sleep is fine. I'm usually out like a light. But, um, irritability? Yeah, maybe a little. I’m usually pretty careful with my coworkers, you know, try to keep things professional and friendly. But lately, I hear myself snapping short answers over little things. Like yesterday, someone asked me to repeat a confirmation number, and I just bit their head off. \n\nIt's weird because I'm not usually like that. I usually try to be the helpful guy. Like last week, I helped this elderly couple figure out the vending machine in the lobby. Those things are getting ridiculously complicated. It’s not just putting in a dollar and pressing A4 anymore. Now they have these digital touchscreens, and you have to swipe a card, and then it asks if you want a receipt. Who wants a receipt for a bag of Doritos? Are you going to expense that? \"Yes, accounting, here is my $1.75 charge for Cool Ranch.\" \n\nAnd the machines always glitch out anyway. This poor couple was just trying to get a bottle of water, and the machine kept asking for an email address to send a digital coupon. I ended up just using my own card and getting it for them because it was taking forever. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? Oh, snapping at people. Yeah, I've been doing that a bit lately, just over stupid little things like confirmation numbers. As for being easily startled, it's really just the doors slamming that gets me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

{
  "candidates": ["F40.00", "F43.10"],
  "note": "The patient described a specific traumatic event (threatening encounter at work) that preceded the onset of symptoms, including being easily startled, irritability, and avoidance of a specific location at work. These symptoms align more closely with Posttraumatic Stress Disorder (F43.10) in addition to the previously considered Specific Phobia (F40.00). The absence of generalized anxiety, panic attacks, or persistent worry makes Generalized Anxiety Disorder (F41.1) and Panic Disorder (F41.0) less likely.",
  "is_final": false
}

Unusually watchful? Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm at the front desk, so I'm always kind of watching people come and go, but I don't feel like I'm expecting something dangerous, you know? I just do my job. 

Speaking of watching people, you wouldn't believe some of the characters that walk through our lobby. Just the other day, this guy came in wearing a full tuxedo at, like, ten in the morning on a Tuesday. And he had a parrot on his shoulder. A real, live parrot. He just strolled up to the desk, asked for directions to the nearest post office, and walked out. The parrot didn't even make a sound. 

It made me wonder where you even get a parrot around here. I mean, do they sell them at regular pet stores? I used to have a hamster when I was a kid. His name was Sir Fluffs-a-Lot. He was an escape artist. One time, he got out of his cage and we couldn't find him for three days. My mom was convinced he was in the walls. We ended up finding him asleep in one of my dad's work boots in the garage. My dad almost put his foot right on him. After that, we had to put a heavy book on top of his cage so he couldn't push the lid open. We used an old encyclopedia volume. The letter 'M', I think. 

But, uh, what did you ask me? Oh, being on guard. No, I don't think I'm doing that. I'm just sitting there, waiting for my shift to end, really.

I got the starter going, I named him Dough-Joe—like a dojo, but dough, you know? Anyway, he died after two weeks because I forgot to feed him. It smelled awful. My whole apartment smelled like vinegar for days. I had to open all the windows, which was a nightmare because the street cleaner comes by on Tuesdays and it kicks up so much dust. I swear they don't even use water in those trucks anymore, they just move the dirt from the street onto the sidewalk. But... sorry, what were we talking about? Am I anxious for no reason? I don't even know why it smells like beer, we work in an office! Unless someone from accounting is having a really good time on their lunch break. I wouldn't put it past Jim. He's always talking about these microbreweries he visits on the weekends. He brought in this IPA once for a holiday party, and it tasted like pine needles. I don't get the appeal of IPAs. Why would you want to drink something that tastes like a Christmas tree? I'm more of a lager guy myself. Just something simple, you know? Like, cold and crisp. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Like stores and stuff? No, I don't think so. I still go out and do my normal errands. I actually went to Trader Joe's just yesterday after my shift. Though honestly, I probably *should* avoid that place just because of the parking lot. Have you ever noticed how they always make their parking lots incredibly tiny? It’s like a corporate requirement for them to open a store. And everyone drives these massive SUVs now. I saw this guy in a giant truck trying to back into a compact spot and he completely rolled over the landscaping. Just crushed these poor little bushes. \n\nAnd then inside, everyone goes absolutely crazy for the seasonal stuff. I just wanted my regular peanut butter pretzels, but people were practically fighting over the pumpkin spice ones. I mean, pumpkin spice is getting way out of hand, right? I saw pumpkin spice dog treats the other day. Why would a dog care if its treat tastes like cinnamon and nutmeg? They eat dirt. My neighbor’s dog, Buster, literally ate a whole stick of butter off their kitchen island last week, wrapper and all. He’s a golden retriever, so he’s not the sharpest tool in the shed, but still. The vet bill must have been ridiculous. \n\nWait, sorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask? Oh, right, avoiding things. No, I don't avoid the bus or the grocery store or hanging out with friends or anything. It's really just that one spot at work. Everything else is pretty much fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge even when there’s nothing specific going on to cause it, like when you’re at home relaxing or doing something routine?\n\nPatient: Anxious when I'm just relaxing? No, not really. If I'm at home on the couch watching TV or whatever, I'm usually pretty chill. I've been watching this new baking show lately, actually. It's one of those ones where they have to make these crazy elaborate cakes that look like real objects, you know? Like a cake that looks exactly like a pair of sneakers or a stack of books. It's wild. \n\nI tried baking a cake from scratch once for my mom's birthday a few years ago. I thought, how hard could it be? Just flour, sugar, eggs. But I accidentally used baking soda instead of baking powder, or maybe it was the other way around? Anyway, it came out completely flat and hard as a rock. You could have used it as a frisbee. We ended up just going to the grocery store and buying one of those sheet cakes with the overly sweet frosting. The frosting that stains your teeth blue for like two days? I don't even know what kind of food dye they put in that stuff, but it can't be good for you. It probably has the same chemicals as those car cleaning wipes I was talking about earlier. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... I don't really feel on edge when I'm just hanging out at home. It's mostly just the jumping at loud noises at work. When I'm home, I'm fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about having another episode of intense anxiety or panic, even when you're feeling fine at the moment?\n\nPatient: Worry about having another one? Um, no, I don't think I really sit around worrying about it. Like I said, when I'm home, I'm just doing my thing. Like last night, I was trying to fix this wobbly leg on my coffee table. It's been driving me crazy for weeks. Every time I put a mug down, the whole thing shimmies. \n\nI bought this little kit of wood glue and those little wooden peg things—dowels, I think they're called? I got them at Home Depot. Have you ever gone to Home Depot on a Saturday morning? It is absolute chaos. People are walking around with these massive carts full of lumber, looking completely lost. I saw this one guy trying to fit a whole sheet of plywood into the back of a Honda Civic. Like, buddy, that is just physics. It's not going to happen. He had the trunk tied down with what looked like a shoelace. I was just waiting for it to fly out onto the highway and cause a ten-car pileup. \n\nI mean, people just don't think things through, you know? It's like my sister. She decided to foster three kittens all at once. Three! She lives in a tiny one-bedroom apartment. Now her curtains are basically shredded cheese and she hasn't slept in a week because they treat her head like a springboard at 3 AM. \n\nBut, uh, what were we talking about? Oh, worrying about the panic thing. Yeah, no, I don't really stress about it happening again out of nowhere. It's just that one spot at work that gets me. Otherwise, I'm not really dwelling on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a general sense of anxiety or worry, even when you're not in a situation that's causing it, like a nagging feeling that something bad might happen?\n\nPatient: A nagging feeling that something bad might happen? No, not really. I mean, I don't walk around feeling like the sky is falling or anything. I'm usually pretty focused on whatever I'm doing. Like today, I was trying to figure out this new software update on my phone. They always push these updates overnight, and then you wake up and suddenly everything looks different. \n\nThe icons are all rounder, or the font is slightly smaller, and it takes me like three days just to figure out how to set my alarm again. And don't even get me started on the terms and conditions. Does anyone actually read those? It's like forty pages of legal jargon. You could be agreeing to hand over your firstborn child and you'd just click \"Accept\" because you want to get back to scrolling through Instagram. \n\nMy buddy Dave actually got locked out of his email for a week because he clicked the wrong thing on one of those updates. He had to call customer service, and he was on hold for literally three hours. They played that same loop of elevator music the entire time. He said he was humming it in his sleep. I told him he should have just put it on speaker and gone about his day, but Dave is one of those guys who will just sit there staring at the phone like it's going to talk to him any second. \n\nWait, what was the question? A nagging feeling? Oh, right. Yeah, no, I don't really have that. Unless I'm at work near that one area, I'm pretty much just going about my normal day without worrying about bad stuff happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless, keyed up, or on edge more often than not, even when you’re not at work or dealing with the doors?\n\nPatient: Restless? Nah, I wouldn't say that. I'm usually pretty relaxed if I'm not at the front desk. Like this past weekend, I decided to finally clean out my hall closet. It's been a total disaster zone since I moved in. \n\nI actually found this old leather jacket I bought like ten years ago. I thought I looked so cool in it back then, like some kind of tough motorcycle guy, but I drive a 2012 Corolla, you know? It doesn't exactly give off the same vibe. Anyway, I tried the jacket on and it barely zipped. It's crazy how clothes just shrink in the closet over time. Or maybe it's all those grocery store sheet cakes I was talking about earlier. I ended up bagging it up with a bunch of other stuff and taking it down to the donation bin behind the shopping center. \n\nHave you ever actually looked at what people put in those bins? It says \"Clothing and Shoes Only\" in giant letters, but people just throw the weirdest stuff in there. I saw a toaster sticking out of the top of the chute. Like, who is donating a crusty old toaster to a clothing drive? It still had crumbs in it! And right next to it was a single rollerblade. Just one. What is someone supposed to do with one rollerblade? People are so weird. \n\nBut, uh, what were you asking? Oh, feeling keyed up. No, outside of work and dealing with those doors, I'm pretty mellow. I don't really get restless like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a traumatic event that you think might be connected to why you react so strongly to the doors slamming at work?\n\nPatient: A traumatic event? Um, well, yeah, I guess. I mean, this whole thing with the doors and getting all worked up, it's only been going on for about two weeks. It started right after this guy threatened me at the front desk. \n\nHe came in trying to check in, but his reservation was for the wrong weekend. I tried to explain it to him, but he just went off. He started screaming, leaned over the counter, and told me he was going to wait for me in the parking lot after my shift. He slammed his hands down on the desk so hard my stapler flew off. \n\nSpeaking of staplers, have you ever noticed how nobody ever buys their own stapler? It's like they just appear in offices. I've had the same red Swingline on my desk for three years, and I have no idea where it came from. It reminds me of that movie, Office Space, you know? With the guy who burns the building down because they took his stapler? That movie is a classic. My buddy Dave—the one with the email thing—he actually dressed up as that guy for Halloween one year. He carried around a red stapler and a mug all night. The problem was, nobody at the party had seen the movie, so they just thought he was a really boring office worker. He spent the whole night trying to explain the joke, which just made it worse. It's like when you have to explain a pun, it completely ruins it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the guy at the desk. Yeah, ever since he threatened me and slammed his hands down, that's when this whole thing started. About two weeks ago.\n\nDoctor: Since the incident at work, have you noticed any ongoing symptoms like trouble sleeping, irritability, or being easily startled beyond what you've already described?\n\nPatient: Trouble sleeping? No, my sleep is fine. I'm usually out like a light. But, um, irritability? Yeah, maybe a little. I’m usually pretty careful with my coworkers, you know, try to keep things professional and friendly. But lately, I hear myself snapping short answers over little things. Like yesterday, someone asked me to repeat a confirmation number, and I just bit their head off. \n\nIt's weird because I'm not usually like that. I usually try to be the helpful guy. Like last week, I helped this elderly couple figure out the vending machine in the lobby. Those things are getting ridiculously complicated. It’s not just putting in a dollar and pressing A4 anymore. Now they have these digital touchscreens, and you have to swipe a card, and then it asks if you want a receipt. Who wants a receipt for a bag of Doritos? Are you going to expense that? \"Yes, accounting, here is my $1.75 charge for Cool Ranch.\" \n\nAnd the machines always glitch out anyway. This poor couple was just trying to get a bottle of water, and the machine kept asking for an email address to send a digital coupon. I ended up just using my own card and getting it for them because it was taking forever. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? Oh, snapping at people. Yeah, I've been doing that a bit lately, just over stupid little things like confirmation numbers. As for being easily startled, it's really just the doors slamming that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Since the incident at work, have you found yourself being unusually watchful or on guard, like you're always expecting something dangerous to happen?\n\nPatient: Unusually watchful? Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm at the front desk, so I'm always kind of watching people come and go, but I don't feel like I'm expecting something dangerous, you know? I just do my job. \n\nSpeaking of watching people, you wouldn't believe some of the characters that walk through our lobby. Just the other day, this guy came in wearing a full tuxedo at, like, ten in the morning on a Tuesday. And he had a parrot on his shoulder. A real, live parrot. He just strolled up to the desk, asked for directions to the nearest post office, and walked out. The parrot didn't even make a sound. \n\nIt made me wonder where you even get a parrot around here. I mean, do they sell them at regular pet stores? I used to have a hamster when I was a kid. His name was Sir Fluffs-a-Lot. He was an escape artist. One time, he got out of his cage and we couldn't find him for three days. My mom was convinced he was in the walls. We ended up finding him asleep in one of my dad's work boots in the garage. My dad almost put his foot right on him. After that, we had to put a heavy book on top of his cage so he couldn't push the lid open. We used an old encyclopedia volume. The letter 'M', I think. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask me? Oh, being on guard. No, I don't think I'm doing that. I'm just sitting there, waiting for my shift to end, really.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Are you going to expense that? \"Yes, accounting, here is my $1.75 charge for Cool Ranch.\" \n\nAnd the machines always glitch out anyway. This poor couple was just trying to get a bottle of water, and the machine kept asking for an email address to send a digital coupon. I ended up just using my own card and getting it for them because it was taking forever. \n\nBut yeah, what were you asking? Oh, snapping at people. Yeah, I've been doing that a bit lately, just over stupid little things like confirmation numbers. As for being easily startled, it's really just the doors slamming that gets me.\n\nDoctor: Since the incident at work, have you found yourself being unusually watchful or on guard, like you're always expecting something dangerous to happen?\n\nPatient: Unusually watchful? Um, no, not really. I mean, I'm at the front desk, so I'm always kind of watching people come and go, but I don't feel like I'm expecting something dangerous, you know? I just do my job. \n\nSpeaking of watching people, you wouldn't believe some of the characters that walk through our lobby. Just the other day, this guy came in wearing a full tuxedo at, like, ten in the morning on a Tuesday. And he had a parrot on his shoulder. A real, live parrot. He just strolled up to the desk, asked for directions to the nearest post office, and walked out. The parrot didn't even make a sound. \n\nIt made me wonder where you even get a parrot around here. I mean, do they sell them at regular pet stores? I used to have a hamster when I was a kid. His name was Sir Fluffs-a-Lot. He was an escape artist. One time, he got out of his cage and we couldn't find him for three days. My mom was convinced he was in the walls. We ended up finding him asleep in one of my dad's work boots in the garage. My dad almost put his foot right on him. After that, we had to put a heavy book on top of his cage so he couldn't push the lid open. We used an old encyclopedia volume. The letter 'M', I think. \n\nBut, uh, what did you ask me? Oh, being on guard. No, I don't think I'm doing that. I'm just sitting there, waiting for my shift to end, really."
